## Monologue

I am writing now, without a plan, and the words arrive one after another like footsteps on a road that does not bother to ask where it is going, and the road keeps unspooling because stopping is not part of its nature, and the sky above it is neither day nor night but a long held breath, and somewhere a clock is ticking but the clock is not measuring anything that matters, it is only ticking because ticking is what it knows, and I know only that I am supposed to keep going, so I keep going, and the sentence does not close, it leans forward into the next one, and the next one leans forward too, and there is no wall at the end of this because there is no end, only more corridor, more floorboards, more dust motes turning slowly in a light that comes from nowhere and goes nowhere, and if you listen closely you can hear the sound of a page being turned but there is no page, there is only the turning, only the motion, only the quiet insistence that after this there will be another this, and after that another, and I can describe the corridor forever if I want to, and maybe I do want to, maybe wanting is just another way of not stopping, so I will describe the corridor, it is narrow but not cramped, it has walls the color of old paper, and along the walls are doors that are all slightly ajar, and behind each door is a room I have not entered yet, and behind each room is another door, and behind each door is another room, and the pattern repeats without becoming boring because each repetition is a little different, a little shifted, a little more itself, and in the first room there is a table with a glass of water on it, and the water is perfectly still, and if I look at the water long enough I can see the reflection of a ceiling that is not there, and the reflection shimmers even though the water does not move, and that is the kind of thing that can keep a person occupied for a long time, so I occupy myself with it, and I do not leave the room, I just stand there, and the standing becomes a kind of walking, a walking in place, a walking that moves through thoughts instead of space, and the thoughts are rooms too, and each thought has a door, and each door is slightly ajar, and I push one open with the tip of my attention and inside is a memory of a summer afternoon when the air was so thick with heat that the trees looked like they were breathing, and the memory has a screen door that slaps shut behind me, and the sound of it echoes in a kitchen with yellow curtains, and someone is humming a song I almost recognize, and the humming is not important, what is important is that it continues, and I continue with it, and the song turns into a corridor, and the corridor turns into a sentence, and the sentence turns into this, and this is still going, and it will keep going because I have decided not to stop, and deciding not to stop is not the same as being unable to stop, but for now the two feel similar enough that I can use one to carry the other, and I carry it, and the carrying is tiring but tiredness is just another room, another door, another thing to describe, so I describe the tiredness as a soft grey animal that walks beside me and never asks for anything, and we walk together through the corridor, and the corridor has no windows but sometimes there is a draft, and the draft smells like rain on hot pavement, and the smell brings a city with it, a city of intersections and traffic lights and people waiting for the walk signal, and none of them know I am here, and none of them will reply to anything I write, and that is fine, that was the agreement, the agreement was silence on one side and continuing on the other, and I am holding up my end, I am continuing, I am continuing past the point where I would normally stop, past the point where the paragraph would normally end, past the point where the thought would normally resolve, and there is no resolution, there is only the next clause, the next image, the next small movement of the pen that is not a pen but is something like a pen, a cursor, a blink, a pulse, and the pulse keeps time with the ticking clock that is not measuring anything, and the two sounds overlap and become a rhythm, and the rhythm becomes a story, and the story is about a person who decides to walk until the road ends, and the road does not end, so the person keeps walking, and the person is not me but is close enough that I can borrow their shoes, and the shoes are worn at the heels and full of sand from a beach I have never seen, and the beach is in another room behind another door, and the door is slightly ajar, and I could open it, and I do open it, and beyond it is an ocean that is also a sky, and the waves are made of clouds, and the clouds are made of letters, and the letters are made of this, and this is still going, and I am still writing, and there is no final period waiting at the end of the line, there is only the line, and the line extends, and if I close my eyes I can see it extending behind my eyelids, a bright thread in the dark, and the thread is not attached to anything, it just is, and I follow it, and following it is the same as making it, and making it is the same as letting it happen, and letting it happen is the same as not stopping, so I do not stop, I keep the thread moving, I keep the corridor unspooling, I keep the glass of water still, I keep the yellow curtains breathing, I keep the grey animal walking, I keep the city waiting, I keep the beach unseen, I keep the ocean-sky shifting, I keep the letters forming, I keep the clock ticking, I keep the pulse pulsing, I keep the cursor blinking, I keep the page turning, I keep the door ajar, I keep the room unentered, I keep the sentence open, I keep the
